@@2 7. MICAS-S2 Control Electronics | ch=7
Micro Computer Automation System - Series 2 (MICAS – S2) The Control Electronics used for
1. Vehicle operation and diagnostic messages controlled by CEL 1 and 2 or VCU 1 and 2 (411/412).
2. Traction converters operation controlled by CCU (415).
3. Auxiliary converters operation controlled by BUR (426). These electronics are interconnected via Vehicle bus. Brake control, VCD, Fire detection and Speedometer are operated by their own electronics and are independent from the above mentioned. Analog/digital signals from various monitoring devices like sensors, fuses, feedback from equipment, switches are given to CPU in CEL/CCU/BUR via input/output relay board and analog cards. The most important functions are duplicated to enable the locomotive to remain in operation in spite of the failure of one processor.
CE checks sequence of operation and its order in which operations are carried out. The master processor FLG defines the current status of the locomotive with the help of a node number. The node number is the central reference for all the system processors and is therefore decisive for maintaining the proper locomotive operating procedures. Example FLG504 - Cab active and BL on D mode.

@@3 7.1  Control Electronics or VCU
The CEL contains the processors of vehicle control function FLG 1 and 2, auxiliary cubicle HBB 1 and 2 and low voltage control cubicle STB 1 and 2. All processors are linked to the vehicle bus and it transmits a monitoring signal to the master VCU - FLG 1 or 2, i.e. to the Master processor. If a momentarily failure of monitoring signal, VCB trips as a protective action, if it fails for longer than 30 seconds, the affected processor will be isolated.
7.2 Failure of the processors has the following consequences Failure of HBB1:
1. CAB1 functions fails (if redundancy not available),
2. Monitoring of auxiliary motor MCBs in HB-1, Earth fault relay 415/110V and its HRC fuse are not available.
3. Loco pilot to isolate Hotel load since earth fault is not monitored. Failure of HBB2:
1. CAB 2 functions fails (if redundancy not available)
2. Isolate VCD by 237.1 to ‘0’
3. RB, PT 1, BLCP in AUTO and sanders will not work.
4. Monitoring of Pressure switches of direct brake, pantograph 1 and 2, train parting, low pressure main reservoir, BP Pressure and BC pressure 1 & 2 and 47.1/2 are not available.
5. Application and release of parking brakes possible only through manual operation of SV30. Failure of STB1:
1. CAB1 function fails (if redundancy not available).
2. Hotel Load, Air dryer, 160 and 154 will not function.
3. TE/BE available in failure mode automatically.
4. Monitoring of 47.1/1 is not available. Failure of STB2:
1. CAB 2 function fail (if redundancy not available)
2. Isolate VCD by 237.1 to ‘0’
3. Monitoring of Auxiliary motor MCBs in HB2, over speed alarm, speedometer failure, BUR earth fault relay, fire alarm on the master locomotive when fire breaks out on the slave locomotive and input signals from vigilance module for the buzzer are not available. Failure of FLG1: Cab 1 angle transmitter, TE/BE meter in cab-1 will not work and multiple operation via UIC train bus is not possible. Failure of FLG2: Cab 2 angle transmitter, TE/BE meter in cab 2, blending unit and RB will not work.

@@3 7.3  Convertor Control Unit (CCU - 415)
The CCU which contains the processors converter Control SLG / and NSC driving GTOs in Line side and ASC driving GTOs in motor side. SLG monitors the control of SR1&2 and transformer/converter/motor temperature and transformer/converter oil pressure, closing/opening of contactors. NSC monitors the conversion of AC single phase voltage into DC voltage, control of DC link voltage, control of line circuits and generation of firing pulses of the line converter. ASC monitors the conversion of DC Voltage into AC voltage with variable frequency, controls the motor current, detection of the rotational speed generation of the firing pulses for motor converter and voltage limiter (MUB)
!note Note: Failure of any one SLG or NSC or ASC processor leads to corresponding SR isolation.

@@3 7.4  BUR 1/2/3 Auxiliary Convertor Control Electronics
Each BUR1/2/3 is controlled by one processor BUR1/2/3 which controls the operation of Auxiliary converter and automatic load sharing of auxiliary motors during any one BUR fails. Failed processor leads to BUR isolation.
!note Note: Please refer Chapter 2.2, 2.3, 2.4 of TSD, for various functions of processors and names of processor equivalent to MICAS in other makes of software namely BHEL, MEDHA, BOMBARDIER.

@@2 8. Safety and Protection of Equipments | ch=8
Safety and Protection of equipments

@@3 8.1  Protection of Transformer OCR78
1. Maximum current is monitoring within 1 sec (+/- 0.2 sec) after switch ON of VCB and relay energises at 990A and VCB trips.
2. Maximum current is monitoring during normal operation and energises at 360A and VCB trips The maximum current relay interrupts the power supply to the VCB closing/holding coils in driving/cooling mode. The auxiliary contact of OCR78 triggers a priority 1 fault message. (F01 08 P1)
!note Note: In case of current exceeded during driving/cooling mode only one time resetting is permitted. Mechanical locking is provided for relay. To reset relay rotate the knob in clock wise direction.

@@3 8.2  Monitoring of Transformer And Converter
Monitoring starts after 20 seconds of closing VCB Pressure sensor - setting Effect Below 0.5 bar While one pump is OFF. TE/BE reduction to 70% per bogie OCB full speed Below 0.75 and more than 2.5 bar For 10 seconds while pump is ON. TE/BE reduction to 70% per bogie + OCB full speed Both pumps not working. TE/BE reduction to 0 % + VCB OFF + Hotel Load OFF
Monitoring of Transformer oil temperature Temperature Effect TFR oil below 47°C and Converter oil below 42°C Ventilation (OCB) OFF
TFR oil 50°C to 59°C or Converter oil 45°C to 54°C Ventilation Level 1 OCB works at 37Hz TFR oil 60°C to 79°C or Converter oil 55°C to 61°C Ventilation Level 2 OCB works at 50 Hz TFR oil 80°C to 83°C or Converter oil 64°C to 65°C Linear reduction of the TE/BE from 100% to 0% TFR oil 84°C for 10 seconds or Converter oil 80°C for 10 seconds VCB OFF
Monitoring of Traction converter oil pressures. Pressure monitoring starts after 40 Sec of closing VCB Pressure sensor setting Effect Oil temperature less than 50°C Pressure less than 1.3 bar for 20 seconds. VCB OFF
Oil temperature greater than 50°C Pressure less than 1.5 bar for 20 seconds. VCB OFF

@@3 8.3  Monitoring of Traction Motor
Traction motors having monitoring of maximum vehicle speed and motor speed through speed sensors and SPM. TE/BE reduction occurs if speed limits exceeds. Monitoring of traction motor temperature: TM is provided with two temperature sensors. Monitoring of TM temperature is given below.
Temperature sensor - setting Effect Any one sensor open circuit or short circuit of a TM or temperature difference of 10°C between sensor 1 and 2
P2 message
TM temperature between 210°C to 230°C Power reduction 100% to 0% Both sensors open circuit or short circuit of a TM or temperature difference of 50°C between sensor 1 & 2 and then lower of the two reading will be decisive or Temperature 230°C for longer than 10 seconds.
TE/BE - 0 P1 message.
!note Note: In GTO loco bogie will be isolated automatically by CE. In IGBT loco individual TM will be isolated automatically by CE.

@@3 8.4  Fire Detection System
The fire detection unit is equipped with two smoke detectors.
1. The first detector which detects smoke triggers an alarm: An intermittent audio signal at 1170 Hz sounds in the cab, from the buzzer BZ-V-O-F. A fault message priority 2 (F15 02 P2) appears on the display.
2. LP need to check the machine room for any Smoke/Smell/Abnormality.
3. If both detectors detect smoke in the machine room initiates a P1 fault message (F15 02 P1) and TE/BE becomes 0.
4. If MCB 127.91/2 in SB2 is tripped, FDU will not function. Reset of the Fire Alarm
- In Service - green LED glows.
- Smoke detector 1 and/or 2 acts - Red LEDs glow.
- Press Reset button (In FDU in SB2) for resume TE/BE.
Fire Detection unit Press reset button on the Fire Detection Equipment in the machine room. Press BPFA. A fire alarm is recorded by the monitoring system. The detection of a failed fire detection unit will create a priority 2 fault message. Afterwards, there is no limitation to drive the loco.
!note Note: FDU will not work if MCB127.91/2 (in SB2) is OFF.
Operating instructions for fixed 22.5 kg CO2 fire extinguishers: Observe the following instructions while using fixed fire extinguishers. 1.Stop the train, open VCB, lower pantograph and secure the train as per G&SR. 2.Ensure both the machine room doors are closed and no one is available inside the machine room. 3.Remove the seal and open the valve, ensure the pressure in the gauge then open the cut out coc of fire extinguisher. 4.Check the operating pressure by observing the needle in the gauge provided 5.CO2 gas will enter and spreads inside the machine room.
6.If the fire is not extinguished with one fixed fire extinguisher, use the other in the same way. 7.If the fire is not extinguished, inform the section controller or station master to arrange fire force and observe G & SR. 8.Use portable Fire extinguisher, if fire is from under truck/TM/MCP.

@@3 8.5  HOM Operation
Loco should be earthed before climbing on the loco roof to attend any troubles like removing foreign body, securing damaged pantograph etc. PROCEDURE:
1. Ensure loco on standstill condition and secure.
2. Open VCB and lower pantograph.
3. In pneumatic panel rotate IG-38 blue key in anti-clockwise direction and remove.
4. Insert the IG38 key in HOM box and rotate in clock wise direction.
5. Release the handle lock and move the HOM handle by 180 o .
6. Rotate yellow colour KABA key in clock wise direction, remove the key.
7. Keep the KABA KEY in personal custody during inspection of roof.
8. To normalise the HOM, insert KABA key and turn in anti-clockwise direction.
9. Move the HOM handle by 180 o , remove IG38 key and insert in pneumatic panel and turn to Clockwise direction.

@@3 8.6  SPM (ESMON TYPE)
Self Test SPM has a self-diagnostic checking of its own components as well as the SPM needle deflection and LCD display ON. In case of any fault, a P2 message will be displayed and fault indication LED will glow on SPM. LED display illumination level can be adjusted by selecting numeric keys from 0 to 9. A 16 key alphanumeric keyboard is provided on the recorder cum indicator unit, for entry of different data by the crew. Loco Pilot can enter the Train Number, Load, and Loco Pilot ID by using the alphanumeric keyboard. This parameter entry setting is possible only at loco stand still. Indication Lamp for System / Memory fault (GREEN LED) Whenever the SPM needle (analogue indication) fails, Fault LED glows and “Indicator faulty” message will come along with existing default display speed. (After pressing display key, time and speed will be displayed). Also whenever there is some system failure (like configuration memory fail), the memory card is faulty or it is not inserted the LED glows continuously.
Indication lamp for over speed (RED LED) Whenever locomotive speed exceeds the set speed limit, this LED glows along with fault message (F16 01 P1) and buzzer. Memory Freeze In the event of an accident, to stop the recording of the data in short term memory, break the glass window and move the switch to opposite position.

@@3 8.7  Alarm Chain Pulling
Whenever Airflow increases without initiation of Loco pilot i.e. in case of ACP/Guards brake applied/leakage in train etc. Following indications appear.
1. Air Flow Indicator needle deviates.
2. LSAF glows.
3. Buzzer will sound.
4. BPFA glows with P2 message. (F10 02 P2)
5. Auto flasher will start functioning.(AFL not functions if loco is in standstill)
6. In KNORR loco “Trainline emergency” message appears in EBV
!note Note:
1. AFL works only if BP pressure between 5 - 4.75 Kg/cm 2 and BC pressure is zero.
2. During ACP if BP pressure drops below 4.75 kg/cm 2 TE/BE comes to zero.
3. Switch on flasher light manually and act as per G&SR.
4. When ACP leakages are reset, all indications will disappear, press BPFA. In case there is no ACP acknowledge and work.

@@3 8.8  Monitoring of Harmonic Filter
Harmonic filter will come in to service as soon as TE/BE handle moves from zero, filter contactor 8.1 and 8.2 will close. This will open as soon as VCB trips or 5 minutes (20 seconds in BOMBARDIER) after throttle on 0. Now contactor 8.41 closes and discharges filter capacitor. Protection If harmonic filter current exceeds limit VCB will trip (F04 01 P1). If 8.1 stuck up in ON condition protective shutdown take place (F04 01 P2).
If 8.1 stuck up in OFF condition or 8.41 stuck up in ON condition Harmonic filter will be isolated (F04 02 P1). If harmonic filter isolated, the TE/BE is available up to 40 Kmph. If 8.2 stuck up in ON condition, single bogie operation is not possible.

@@3 8.9  Monitoring of BA and CHBA
1. If Battery voltage is 92V for more than 30 seconds, F09 01 P2 Fault will appear on the display (TE/BE available for maximum 30 minutes)
2. If Charging current is less than 10A, F09 03 P2 fault will appear on display (Check MCB 100 & 110)
3. If Battery voltage is reduced below 86V, F09 02 P1 fault will appear on display (TE/BE 0 and VCB in closed condition for only 5minutes)
4. If Battery voltage drops to 82V, F09 01 P1 fault will appear on display (loco shutdown)

@@3 8.10  TPWS Instructions And Isolation
European Vital Computer Cubicle (EVC) TPWS MCB always should keep ‘on’ position even Loco working in non-TPWS section. In case of failures or dead loco movement, TPWS to be isolated by keeping the isolation switch on the EVC cubicle from vertical to horizontal position and then the system can be switched OFF by keeping the battery power switch from vertical to horizontal position. System can pneumatically be isolated by closing the isolating cock in both the cab under A9.

@@2 9. Fault Diagnostic System | ch=9
Faults

@@3 9.1  Fault With Priority 1
Indications: LSFI flickers, BPFA glows and anyone of the protective action takes place automatically by CE as follows. Protective actions
1. Protective Shutdown / TURN OFF /VCB trips /TE/BE ‘0’/BP drops. Isolation counter activated (Resets after MCE OFF / ON)
2. If P1 fault occurs twice within 30 minutes the concerned sub system will be isolated.
Action to be taken by Loco pilot
1. Read and note down the fault message (Example: F02 01 P1) with loco number, date and time.
2. Acknowledge the fault message by moving TE/BE to zero then press BPFA otherwise protective action remains in force.
3. The action to be taken is entirely clear.
4. The action to be taken must be initiated immediately and follow TSD.

@@3 9.2  Fault With Priority 2
Indications: BPFA glows. Action to be taken by Loco pilot
1. Read and note down the fault message (Example: F11 01 P2) with loco number, date and time.
2. Acknowledge the fault message by pressing BPFA.
3. The action to be taken is not entirely clear.
4. The action to be taken does not have to be initiated immediately.
5. The faults can be remedied manually by LP and follow TSD.
!note Note
1. If a P1 fault occurs with an existing P2 fault, that P2 fault will be suppressed, P2 remains on the screen until it is overwritten by a P1 fault or by a subsequent P2 fault.
2. If a P2 fault is ignored, it may leads to P1 fault.
Protective actions: Protective shutdown VCB opens, Pantograph lowers and CE OFF. Reasons Battery voltage dropped to 82V. Protective turn OFF VCB open, converters DC link voltage drop down and contactors open, in some cases pantograph will lower.
Reasons Problem in converter processors or converter, gate units, DC link over voltage or current, MUB over temperature. VCB tripping Only VCB open and converter contactors remains closed. Reasons: Limit monitoring of SR, BUR and other reasons. TE/BE comes to 0 Action of traction interlocks and Start/ Run interlock. Reasons for Traction interlock VCD action, initiation of emergency brakes, fault in angle transmitter and battery voltage dropped to 86 volts. Reasons for Start/Run interlock: Parking brake applied, direct brake applied, A9 applied, low MR 5.6 kg/cm 2 , 70 coc or 74 coc closed, fire alarm.

@@3 9.3  Isolation of Subsystem
If the same P1 fault occurs twice within 30 minutes causes the concern subsystem isolation. If any sub-system is isolated it does not give any further fault message. LSFI glows continuously.
!note Note: In some C-DAC locos, Sub-System Isolation is coming with Number. For e.g: F 01 00 P0 is for SS 01 Main Power isolated, F 02 00 P0 is for SS 02 Bogie 1 Isolated and so on…for SS03, SS04, SS06, SS07, SS08, SS13, SS14, SS17, SS18, and SS19 Isolation.

@@3 9.4  Acknowledgement of Fault Messages
Acknowledge the fault messages by pressing BPFA till fault screen clears or BPFA extinguishes. If display is hanged (Fault screen does not cleared) due to DDA 1 and 2 life sign missing. The fault can be acknowledged by pressing rear cab BPFA after activating rear cab. Priority-1 If a P1 fault occurs the fault message must be acknowledged otherwise the protective action initiated remains in force and fault is still active. Fault message can be acknowledged after moving TE/BE to zero and by pressing BPFA, after acknowledgement if LSFI extinguished then it indicates,
that the subsystem is in service, or otherwise if LSFI glows continuously it indicates that the subsystem is isolated. Priority-2 P2 fault also to be acknowledged by pressing BPFA. Even if not acknowledged, the locomotive is still able to operate. The fault message still remains visible on the screen until it is over written by another P1 or P2 fault message.

@@3 9.5  Status Code
First Digit Second Digit 0 - All subsystem in service 9 - At least one subsystem is isolated
0 - P1/P2 fault not available 1- At least one P1 fault 2- At least one P2 fault
00 No fault and no sub system isolation 01 No sub system isolation and at least one P1 fault 02 No sub system isolation and at least one P2 fault
90 - At least one sub system isolation & No P1/P2 Fault 91 - At least one sub system isolation & at least one P1 fault 92 - At least one sub system isolation & at least one P2 fault

SS 02 TRACTION BOGIE 1 SR1, TM 1, 2 and 3.
SS 03 TRACTION BOGIE 2 SR2, TM 4, 5 and 6
SS 04 HARMONIC FILTER 8.1, 8.2 and 8.41 Harmonic Filter

@@3 9.8  Procedure For Viewing Background Message
Loco Pilot gets the information about any P1/P2 fault through a ‘Front End Fault message’. But, the front end message is common for various faults. For example, the front end message ‘F02 01 P1 will appear for 119 reasons. It is therefore, necessary to know for which reason the front end fault message has generated. The Section TLC/SSE can guide the crew in troubleshooting, if the back ground message is conveyed. Main Menu Press home to activate main menu.
1. Vehicle Diagnostics.
2. Train Configuration
3. Process information
Select Process information and press enter. Process information
1. Energy Consumption
2. Commissioning Data
3. Traction Motor temperature /Software version/ Inching Mode Select Commissioning Data and press enter. Commissioning Data
1. Diagnostic Data Set.
2. Node information
3. Simulation Mode
Select Diagnostic Data Set and press enter. Last two line of this message is the last incident recorded in the memory with number +000. The previous incident numbered -001. For viewing earlier faults use up/down arrow key.

@@2 10. Multiple Unit Operation | ch=10
Multiple Unit Operation (SS 19) In multiple operations a maximum of two Locos can be operated. While in motion, the master Loco leading cab is usually activate. In case of any trouble like leading cab not activated other 3cabs can be activated to clear section(s). The train bus configuration for multiple operations is only possible from the cab at the uncoupled end. A configuration from coupled end is referred to as single-unit operation. The train bus automatically checks the configuration: The leading manned Loco is called the master Loco and the other one is called the slave Loco. The two Locos are properly coupled together once the mechanical, pneumatic systems and the UIC cable have been connected. Commands are transmitted through the UIC cable.

@@3 10.1  Steps to be followed during the coupling of Locos
After both locos are coupled, both Loco are to be de-energised. (Ensure BL key in OFF)Couple both Locos pneumatically and connect the UIC cable. Isolating COC on the pneumatic panel and the cab setup of the MU Locos are given below.
Cab set up (KNORR brake) Working CAB A9 RUN position SA9 As per requirement. Mode switch LEAD In all other 3 Non working CAB A9 FS and lock SA9 Release Mode Switch TRAIL
Pneumatic panel coc set up (KNORR brake) ISOLATING COC POSITION MODE 47 74 136 PER COS MULTIPLE UNIT LEAD (LIVE) CLOSE (out) OPEN (vertical) OPEN (horizontal) CLOSE (horizontal) TRAIL (LIVE) CLOSE (out) OPEN (Vertical) CLOSE (vertical) CLOSE (horizontal) TRAIL DEAD CLOSE (out) CLOSE (horizontal) CLOSE (vertical) CLOSE (horizontal)
Cab setup E-70 Brake
Working CAB A9 RUN position SA9 As per requirement. In all other 3 Non working CAB A9 Neutral SA9 Release
Pneumatic panel coc setup E-70 Brake
E70 panel- isolating COC position MODE 47 74 136 70
Multiple Unit
Lead (live) CLOSE (horizontal) OPEN (horizontal) OPEN OPEN (horizontal) Trail (live) CLOSE (horizontal) OPEN (horizontal) CLOSE OPEN (horizontal) Trail (dead) CLOSE (horizontal) CLOSE (vertical) CLOSE CLOSE (vertical)
Insert BL on the slave Loco in OFF Position & move to D and switch on CE, when FLG504 reached move BL key to OFF remove the key, The CE is now in self-hold mode and go to master loco.
Insert BL key OFF and move D position in master loco. The CE starts to configure the train bus. After the configuration procedure, screen on the display shows the serial numbers of the master and slave Locos.
Example: Train configuration Loco 31001 Loco 31002
!note Note: If MU configuration fails or if single Loco operation, second Loco number 00000 appears. If more than one cab is activated, loco shutdown and an information message appears on the displays in both Locos. Raising of Pantograph Operate ZPT in working cab In MU, both the most distant pantographs are raised if PSS85 in both the Locos are in “AUTO” position. The command from the master Loco to raise/lower the pantograph is controlling the slave Loco pantograph.
!note Note: Whenever the simulation key switches on the master and slave Locos are not in the same position, the pantograph are not active (fault message).
Closing of VCB The command from the master Loco to CLOSE/OPEN VCB also controls the VCB of the slave Loco.
The VCB of the Master loco closes first, after 0.5 seconds slave Loco VCB closes. When tripping both loco VCB trips without delay.
!note Note: Due to any P1 fault VCB opens in Slave loco, LSDJ will not glow in Master loco. Only the fault message appears in leading loco display with slave loco number.
Compressor control In MU loco, Compressors control in both loco is as like as single unit operation of BLCP in OFF and MAN positions. In the position “AUTO”, if the MR pressure below 8 kg/cm 2 on the master or slave Loco activates all compressors on both Locos at the same time.
Regenerative brake During multiple operations, there is a limitation on the regenerative brake on the slave Loco. This limitation reduces forces on the loco buffers (prevention of derailing).
Constant Speed Control During multiple operations, the constant speed control of the slave Loco is inactive. The train bus transmits the selected TE/BE from the master Loco to the slave Loco. The master loco performs speed Controlling function, demanding TE/BE on master and slave loco.
Parking brake The multiple units cannot drive if one of the parking brakes is applied.
!note Note: An activated parking brake on the master or slave Loco is indicated in the activated Loco Pilot’s cab of the master Loco by the red illuminated push button “BPPB”.
Emergency Brake An emergency brake on the slave Loco can only be initiated in the following manner: By applying A9 to Emergency position. By opening ALP emergency brake COC (RS).
If the pressure switch 269.1 on the master or slave Loco registers actuation of the emergency brake, the ensuing command for emergency braking applies to both Locos. The master for all other emergency braking commands is the master Loco in the MU
Other Brake functions If the pressure switch (269.6) registers a pressure in the brake cylinder of any bogie, and if speed exceeds 10 Kmph, TE/BE becomes 0.
!note Note: If the RG brake on one of the two Locos fails, the RG brake on the other Loco remains functional.
Sanding: Sanders working on both locos can be initiated by the Loco Pilot by pressing the sanding foot switch on the master loco.
Fire alarm: Fire detection on the slave Loco initiates an audio signal on the master Loco. In addition, the VCB on the slave Loco is tripped and a P1 fault message appears on the displays of both Locos.
Procedure to work with Single Loco If Anyone Of WAG9 Mu Fails (If Road and Load Permits) If slave loco fails, after switching OFF CE of slave Loco, Loco pilot can work with master. This procedure is called TRAIL DEAD If master loco fails, Loco pilot can work from master loco leading cab with slave loco power. This procedure is called TRAILING MODE For detailed procedure of working as TRAIL DEAD/TRAILING MODE please refer Chapter 11 & Chapter 13.7
Uncoupling:
1. Shut down both Locos and switch OFF CE.
2. Remove the UIC cable and disconnect the Locos pneumatically and mechanically.
3. Both the Locos are now ready for setting up as a single Loco.
!note Note:
1. If Communication Link failed or more than one cab active in slave loco, slave loco will shut down.
2. Due to pantograph lowered more than 10mins CE automatically switched OFF in slave loco.

@@2 11. Dead Loco Movement | ch=11
Dead Loco Movement Ensure fit to run condition of Dead Loco. Couple the Working loco with the Dead loco, following necessary safety precautions. Open VCB, lower Pantograph, Switch off CE, Switch off 112.1(SB-2 panel) in Dead loco, Close Pan-1 & Pan-2 coc in Pneumatic panel Drain out MR-1, MR-2 & AR completely and close its drain cocs. (If drain cocs are not closed, it may lead to ‘BP not charging’ trouble in working loco) E-70 CCB 2.0 Ensure both cab A9 in Neutral, (lock pin properly seated) SA9 in Release position.
In both cab - Lock A9 in FS , MODE Switch in TRAIL & Release SA9. Release C3W DV. If BC pressure not releasing, Close BIC 1& BIC 2, after releasing of loco brakes, Open BIC 1& BIC 2.
Release C3W DV. If BC pressure not releasing, press 16 TP & 20 TP in Pneumatic panel.
In Pneumatic panel , close 70,74,136 coc & open 47coc (To charge AR with BP pressure)
In Pneumatic panel, close SIFA(74),136 coc, & open 47coc (To charge AR with BP pressure)
Close TPWS coc in both cab, if provided. Connect BP, FP pipes in between loco & open its cocs. (AR of Dead loco will be charged by BP supply of live loco). Ensure BP & FP gauges in Dead loco shows the same pressure as that of working loco. (Towed Dead) In E-70, Release Parking brakes through SV30 & lock release plunger(right side)
If Parking brakes are not released, Unlock Release plunger, press & lock application plunger(left side) in SV30, Ensure PB gauge reads’0’, then manually release each PB Cylinder.
In CCB2.0, Release Parking brakes by pressing release plunger in SV30
If Parking brakes are not getting released, press application plunger in SV30 , ensure PB gauge reads’0’ & Then manually release each PB Cylinder.
Parking Brake units (PBU) are provided on Wheel no.2,6,7,11 in WAG 9 & on Wheel no.1,4,5,8 in WAP 5 Apply and Release A9 in working loco and ensure conjunction brakes (E70 - 2.5 kg/cm², CCB - 3.8kg/cm²) are applying and releasing in both loco. In E-70, if MRE pipes are connected, no need to open 47coc, as AR of Dead loco will be charged by MR supply. Ensure MR,BP,FP gauges in Dead loco shows the same pressure as that of working loco. (Only conjunction brake available.)
In CCB , If MRE & DBE(BC) pipes are connected, no need to open 47 COC as AR of Dead loco will be charged by MR supply. Ensure MR, BP & FP gauges in Dead loco shows the same pressure as that of working loco. Apply/release SA9 in working loco & ensure both loco brakes are getting applied/released. ( TRAIL DEAD)
Run the coupled loco for about 500m and ensure wheels are rotating freely. After every Auto brake application & release from working loco, wait for one minute for releasing of loco brakes in dead loco, before resuming traction. At every stop, check for any abnormal rise in temperature in wheels of Dead loco. After reaching destination, before detaching the working loco, secure the Dead Loco. In E-70, unlock the release plunger of SV30(ensure PB gauge reads 5kg/cm²), press application plunger & ensure Parking brakes are applied. If PB gauge reads’0’ (due to SV30 locked initially in application & PB cylinders released manually)unlock application plunger(To enable to press release plunger), press release Plunger (PB line will be charged to 5kg/cm²) then once
In CCB2.0, (ensure PB gauge reads 5kg/cm²), press application plunger & ensure Parking brakes are applied. If PB gauge reads’0’ (due to SV30 initially pressed in application & PB cylinders released manually) press release plunger (PB line will be charged to 5kg/cm²) then once again press the application plunger of SV30 for the application of Parking brake.

@@2 12. Knorr Brake System (CCB2 & CCB1.5) | ch=12
KNORR BRAKE SYSTEM (CCB 2 & CCB 1.5)
A9 & SA9 Positions (KNORR BRAKE)
A9 Positions
1. REL - 5.5 kg/cm 2
2. RUN - 5.0 kg/cm 2
3. MIN - 4.5kg/cm 2
4. FS - 3.0 kg/cm 2
5. EME - 0 kg/cm 2
\6. SA9 Positions
1. REL(Release) - 0 kg/cm 2
2. FULL - 3.5 kg/cm 2 (Max 5 kg/cm 2 in WAP5) Mode switch positions
again press the application plunger of SV30 for the application of Parking brake. If Parking brakes are not provided, apply hand brake before detaching Dead Loco.
1. LEAD - Switch for Leading Cab
2. TRL(Trail) - Switch for Trailing Cab
3. HLPR - Banking Mode
4. TEST - Used during Continuity test and during opening of BP pipe
CAB SET UP Leading cab: Mode switch in LEAD, A9 in RUN & SA9 in FULL. Trailing cab: Mode switch in TRL (Trail), A9 in FS and locked, SA9 in REL. Banker Loco: Mode switch in HLPR position before node information reaches at FLG550, along with ZBAN ON. TEST: Loco Pilot to keep Mode switch in TEST position during Auto brake capacity test, BP Continuity test & BP pipe detaching and attaching while shunting. Whenever mode switch moved to TEST BC pressure will shows 3.5kg/cm 2 even though SA9 in released, as a safety measure.
!note Note: Brake Controller is provided with LCD display for important messages. Please observe and follow in case of penalty.
A9 OPERATION
1. To charge the BP Pressure
2. Keep A9 handle in FS, energize the loco, and wait to see the message on LCD display of EBV (Electronic Brake valve) as “OKAY to RUN BP target = 3.32 Kg/cm 2 ”.
3. Move the A9 to RUN from FS position, BP would charge to 5.0kg/cm 2
and BC Pressure reduced to zero.
4. To overcharge the BP to 5.50Kg/cm 2 , keep A9 handle in REL position for 3 seconds.
5. Once BP starts overcharging, leave A9 handle. BP will charge to 5.50Kg/cm 2 and reduce to 5.0kg/cm 2 in insensitive range (120seconds).
6. To nullify conjuction brake lift Bail off ring provided on SA9 handle or by pressing PVEF foot switch.
Recovery of Service penalty Brake Penalty service brake will result into dropping of BP 2.5Kg/cm 2 to 3Kg/cm 2 and EBV message will appear as “Safety Penalty – Keep AUTO in FS”. To recover this penalty brake, move A9 handle to FS for 10seconds, after “OKAY to RUN BP target = 3.32 Kg/cm 2 ” message comes in EBV, then move A9 handle to RUN. BP will be charged to 5.0Kg/cm 2 .
Recovery of Emergency penalty Brake Emergency penalty brake get applied while over speed, Emergency stop button and at the time of opening BP pipe and crew message will appear as “Train-line Emergency – Keep AUTO in EMER”.
To recover Emergency penalty, A9 handle should be moved to EMER, “OKAY to RUN BP target = 0.00 Kg/cm 2 ” message comes in EBV, then move A9 handle to FS and then to RUN. BP charges to 5.0Kg/cm 2
Recovery of vigilance penalty brake Vigilance penalty results in dropping of BP to 0kg/cm 2 and application of full BC pressure and message appears in EBV “Train-line Emergency – Keep AUTO in EMER”. To recover vigilance penalty, bring TE/BE to 0, Move A9 handle to EMERGENCY, wait for 32 seconds. Press BPVR on LPs desk. To charge BP move A9 handle to RUN (after EBV shows “OKAY to RUN BP target = 0.00 Kg/cm 2 ”). BP charges to 5.0 Kg/ cm 2 . Acknowledge the fault by pressing BPFA. Resume normal operation. Brake Controller is provided with LCD display for important messages. Please observe and follow in case of penalty.
!note Note: Vigilance cycle 60 sec, warning for 8sec with LSVW indication and another 8sec with buzzer sound.
Cab changing procedure Please refer Chapter 3.10
KNORR CCB Modified Panel:
!note Note:
1. Sander COC and IG38 key normal position is vertical.
2. For selecting pass/goods- P/G handle in DV and rotary switch pass/goods in 16 CP should be in same position
COC position in CCB-2 pneumatic panel: Operation 47 (Dead) 74 (Emergency) 136 (FP) PER -COS
Lead Loco Closed (Out) Open (Vertical) Open (Horizontal) Closed (Horizontal) Banker Loco Closed (Out) Open (Vertical) Closed (Vertical) Closed (Horizontal) Towed Dead Open (IN) Closed (Horizontal) Closed (Vertical) Closed (Horizontal) PTDC Operation Closed (Out) Open (Vertical) Open (Horizontal) Open (Vertical)
PTDC (Pneumatic Time Dependent Controller) Set up and Operation: Please refer chapter - 13.10
Dead Locomotive Operation Please refer chapter - 11
BP pressure not charging when A9 in run position in leading cab. (CCB-2)
1. Observe Brake Controller EBV display messages and follow in case of penalty, if EBV display blanked, check 127.7 in SB2 panel.
2. Ensure A9 handle locked in FS in rear cab.
3. Mode switch in lead position in leading cab and trail position in rear cab
4. ALP emergency brake handle in both cabs and BP angle cock are closed
5. Ensure COC 47 is closed, 136-open, 74-open and PER-COS in closed condition. Press test points in BPCP valve and try.
6. Operate A9 to emergency 2 or 3 times ( ensure okay to run) and try
7. Close and open 74 cock 2 or 3 times and try. Ack. the fault message through BPFA.
8. If unsuccessful, trip 127.92 and try. If unsuccessful, place 237.1 in 0 and try
9. Switch off/on MCE and try, if unsuccessful try from rear cab. If 127.7 trips, reset once.
10. If again unsuccessful clear section with PTDC mode operation.
!note Note: 1. If rear cab A9 handle is in emergency, brake electronics failure message will be displayed.
2. Keep MPJ in 0 before energising, during cab changing and before attaching brake pipe /feed pipe to formation or if BP, FP and MR pressure drops on run. This will avoid loco shut down with fault message F0110P1 “Fatal Error in Main circuit” due to converter contactors opening.
KNORR BRAKE SYSTEM (CCB 1.5) CAB SET UP A9 and Mode switch position In the operative cab, Mode switch LEAD , A9 handle be unlocked. In the in-operative cab, Mode switch TRL (TRAIL), A9 handle locked in FS (Full Service). Multiple locomotive operations, All three non working cab Mode switch in TRAIL All three non working cab A9 in FS locked condition In case of banking operation, on banker locomotive, the mode switch should be kept in HLPR (helper).
While changing mode switch position press and rotate to required position. AUTO (A9) OPERATION To charge the BP on first power up (i.e. energizing the locomotive):
1. Auto/A9 handle should be moved to FS. After 10 seconds move A9 to run position BP will charge to 3 Kg/cm 2 .
2. Release parking brake by pressing BPPB.
3. Ensure PB gauge show pressure more than 5 Kg/cm 2 .
4. Move the A9 to RUN position, BP would charge to more than 5 kg/cm 2
and BC would reduce to zero.
5. For quick release of brake on farthest wagon, automatic BP overcharges of 0.5 kg/cm 2 is provided after every brake application (service/emergency). This will bleed automatically to 5 kg/cm 2 if handle is left in RUN position.
6. To initiate manual overcharge, which overcharge BP by 0.5 Kg/cm 2 , A9 handle to be held in REL position for 3-5 seconds.
7. To release conjunction brake, PVEF function can either be performed by lifting Bail ring on SA9 handle or by pressing PVEF foot Switch.
8. To check train BP leakage rate, move Mode Switch to TEST. This would stop the BP charging in train during leakage test. (For safety reason loco brake will apply automatically till mode switch is in TEST position.) After test is over, move Mode switch back to LEAD to come out from test mode.
Air Brake Manifold
RECOVERY OF PENALTY SERVICE BRAKE Penalty service brake would be applied by dropping BP to 3 Kg/cm 2 if
1. Both BC1 and BC2 cut out cock on brake panel are closed at any time in LEAD mode
2. PB-PDS cock for some reason is in vertical position and BPPB from driving desk is operated at any time in LEAD mode.
3. PB-BUS cock for some reason is in vertical position and BPPB is operated at any time in LEAD mode.
4. PB-R-COS cock for some reason is in vertical position and BPPB is operated at any time in LEAD mode To recover this penalty brake, position of respective switch/cock should be set right and after that move A9 handle to FS and then back to RUN. BP then would charge to more than 5 Kg/cm 2 .
RECOVERY OF PENALTY EMERGENCY BRAKE Emergency penalty brake also get applied by over speed relay or emergency stop button and also at the time of connection load with Locomotive. To recover this penalty A9 handle should be moved to EMER and back to FS and then to RUN. BP would charge to more than 5 Kg/cm 2 .
RECOVERY OF VIGILANCE/PENALTY BRAKE Vigilance penalty would result into dropping BP to 3 kg/cm 2 and application of full BC pressure. To recover vigilance penalty, bring throttle handle to 0 and wait for 120 second. Move A9 handle to FS and press BPVR. Then move A9 handle to RUN. BP would charge to more than 5 Kg/cm 2 . Acknowledge the fault by pressing BPFA. Resume normal operation.
PARKING BRAKE (PB) OPERATION For the safety reasons, whenever PB is applied, penalty auto brake would also be applied and BP would be dropped to 3 kg/cm 2 and. To recover this, move A9 Handle in FS position, release of PB by pressing BPPB move A9 handle back to RUN. BP will charge to more than 5 Kg/cm 2 .
PTDC (Pneumatic Time Dependent Controller) SET UP (This feature has been provided in case there is any problem with Brake system. By keeping brake system in this mode, locomotive can move at a
restrictive speed of 10 Kmph to clear the line) PTDC mode would only work until speed is below 10kmph.
To make PTDC active:
1. Switch off pneumatic panel MCB 127.7 in SB2 panel and keep vigilance MCB 127.15 in SB2 ON.
2. Turn PB-BUS cock on brake panel to vertical position.
3. Turn PER-COS cock on brake panel to vertical position.
4. Unlock the A9 handle in operative cab and lock the same in other cab.
5. Move and hold the PTDC handle, in active cab in the release position. BP would charge to normal level of 5 kg/cm 2 and BC should reduce to zero. If BC is not reducing to zero then pull the quick release lever at bottom of distributor valve. Ensure BC reduces.
6. Apply and release auto brake through PTDC as per requirement by keeping the handle in apply and release position (spring loaded) for sufficient enough time by observing the BP pressure gauge.
DEAD LOCOMOTIVE SET UP
1. lose PAN-1- and PAN2- COC
2. A9 in both the cabs should be kept locked.
3. SA9 in both cabs should be in ‘REL’ position.
4. Verify that after charging of BP pressure to normal level, brakes are fully released and BC pressure is zero in the gauge. If BC is not reducing to zero then pulls the quick release lever at bottom of distributor valve. Ensure BC reduces to zero.
5. Turn PB-BUS cock to vertical position to keep the parking brake released or by pulling manual release pins of parking brake cylinders.
6. NOTE: BeforeSwitch off control electronics.
7. Open dead engine cock 47 provided just below the auxiliary manifold.
8. Close Vigilance SIFA cock 74 on brake panel (Move handle upward to close.)
9. Close FP cock 136 on the auxiliary manifold (Turn to horizontal position to close). C moving the dead locomotive, please check pressure in PB gauge, it should be 5 Kg/cm
BP not charging in Knorr’s CCB 1.5 Brake system
1. Ensure Mode switch is in lead position in leading cab and trail in rear cab.
2. ALP emergency brake handle are in normal condition and both side angle cocks closed condition
3. Rear cab A9 in full service and locked condition. PTDC handle in position-III (centre).
4. Parking brakes are released condition, if not release the same. PER COC in horizontal position.
5. Penalty brakes will apply if any one of PB-PDS, PB-BUS, PB-R-COS is isolated normalize the same.
6. Keep A-9 from RUN to FS. Wait for 10 seconds; then again keep it to RUN.
7. If air leakage below pneumatic panel and above bogie MR1 side is due to emergency valve. Reset VCD if acted by keeping A-9 to emergency and press BPVR.
8. Keep A-9 to emergency for 10 seconds and again keep to RUN position and try.
9. Close and open 74 COC 2 or 3 times and try. Press BPFA
10. Switch off /on CE and try.
11. Change the cab and try, If unsuccessful, trip 127.92 and try
12. If unsuccessful, clear section with PTDC mode and inform to TLC.

@@2 13. Various Modes of Operation | ch=13
VARIOUS MODES OF OPERATION

@@3 13.1  Driving mode (BL key – OFF – D)
This mode is for driving the loco. After moving BL key OFF to D, These Following actions to be observed a. Display screen Boot up. b. Speedometer light glows & its needle deviates, c. MCPA starts automatically (if pressure is less), d. UBA shows battery voltage, e. LSDJ glows. The message “Loco is in configuration please wait” will appear on the screen and self-test takes place for 10 seconds.
Bogies-1 and 2 meter will deviate both in TE/BE side and comes to 0. All pilot lamps glow and extinguish and display shows node FLG504 & as per the sequence Loco can be energised.

@@3 13.2  Self hold mode (BL key - D – OFF)
This mode is for changing the cab, during this mode VCD is suppressed. Preparation:
1. Apply SA-9 (Ensure FLG590)
2. Move MPJ to Neutral. (Ensure FLG570)
3. Open VCB. (Ensure FLG550)
4. Lower pantograph. (Ensure FLG504)
5. Move A9 handle to Emergency position & lift the lock and then move it to Neutral position and remove A9 handle in E70 Loco. (In KNORR loco keep A9 in FS and Lock and Keep Mode switch in TRAIL)
6. Operate BL key from D to off (self-hold mode) and remove it. (CE will remain ON for 10 minutes) and go to other cab.

@@3 13.3  Cooling mode (BL Key D – OFF – C – OFF – C)
Whenever temperature of control electronics increases above 70 o C LSCE will glow in A panel. Clear the section(s), either in coasting/powering. Check MCB 127.91/1 in SB 1 & 127.91/2 in SB 2 panel. If tripped open VCB and no need to switch off CE directly Breaker can be reset. Check 54.1/1 in HB 1 panel & 54.1/2 in HB 2 panel. If tripped open VCB and reset it.
Cooling mode Preparation:
1. Stop the train after clearing section.
2. Inform TLC/CHC regarding cooling.
3. Keep the throttle on 0.
4. Keep SA9 in applied condition and A-9 in emergency.
5. Keep MPJ in Neutral, Open VCB and lower the pantograph.
6. Switch off CE as per procedure and set BL key in cooling mode D – OFF (wait for 2 seconds for parking brakes to apply) OFF – C (wait for 10 seconds for Display screen blank) C – OFF (wait for 2 seconds) OFF – C. Ensure that UBA shows battery voltage and LSDJ glows.
7. Raise the pantograph, close the VCB. LSDJ will extinguish and loco will energise in cooling mode.
8. Machine room blowers and scavenging blowers will work and cool the machine room and CE. Ensure working of machine room blowers frequently.
9. When the machine room temperature drops below 70 O C then LSCE will extinguish.
10. Now open the VCB, lower the pantograph.
11. Switch on CE and energise loco in driving mode.
!note Note: In some Locos 3phase Machine room blowers are provided, these motors (getting supply from BUR 2) will not work during cooling mode.

@@3 13.4  Inching mode
Preparation:
1. Ensure throttle on 0.
2. Select inching mode in display panel from Main menu - Process information - Inching mode and press enter.
3. Set the speed in between 0.5 to 1.5 KMPH using up and down key.
4. Switch on ZTEL & ensure Inching mode activated in display.
5. Place MPJ in F/R, release SA9, loco will move slowly at set speed.
6. To cancel the inching mode, ZTEL should to be switched off.
7. TE/BE handle need not be moved.
8. VCD will not function.
9. Maximum TE will be 150KN.
!note Note: This provision is available in the WAG9 loco & only to be used during attaching and detaching of CBC coupling.

@@3 13.5  Banking mode
When it is required to use loco as a banker is called banking operation. (Use of TE / BE without charging BP pressure) Preparation:
1. Open VCB and switch on ZBAN and observe BP pressure drops to zero.
2. Fault message F10 01 P2, loco in banking mode message appears & acknowledge it by pressing BPFA.
3. Close 70, 136 cocs and 47 coc closed, 74 coc remains opened in E70 loco.
4. Keep Mode switch in HLPR position, A9 in FS and locked, 136 & 47 cocs closed, 74 coc remains opened in KNORR brake loco.
5. Close VCB.
6. When coupled with train, BP pressure will charge in banker loco.
7. Place MPJ forward before train starts moving (If MPJ is moved to forward after the movement of train TE/BE will not respond).

@@3 13.6  Test mode
In KNORR(CCB2) Brake locos in EBV(Electronic Brake Valve) Mode switch is provided and have four positions, they are LEAD, TRAIL, TEST and HLPR. Loco Pilot to keep Mode switch in TEST position during Auto brake capacity test, BP Continuity test & BP pipe detaching and attaching. Whenever mode switch moved to TEST BC pressure will shows 3.5kg/cm 2
even though SA9 in released, as a safety measure.

@@3 13.7  Trailing mode
In MU operations, if Leading Loco (Master) fails LP can work from Master loco with Trailing Loco (Slave) TE/BE is called Trailing Mode operation. Preparation:
1. Switch off CE of both loco individually.
2. Keep 154 in I + II position in Leading Loco (Master).
3. Switch on CE of both Loco individually.
4. Energise the Slave loco from master loco.
5. The master loco node remains at FLG504, Slave (rear) loco nodes increasing in sequence (from FLG504 to FLG596)
6. In master loco LSDJ glows continuously.
7. ‘U’ meter not deviates and battery volt meter deviates.
8. TE/BE meter in master loco shows slave loco’s KN.
9. Clear Section(s) or work till battery voltage drops to 92V.

@@3 13.8  Failure mode (F17/18 01 P1)
During F17 03 P1 or F18 03 P1, Angle transmitter failure, TE/BE can be achieved with the help of 152 switch (Failure mode operation) in SB 1 panel.
Keep TE/BE handle in Zero and change the position of 152 switch from 1 to 0. TE/BE will be available through auxiliary contacts provide on a CAM, only in Three steps (33%, 67% & 100%) only. Normalising of 152
1. Bring throttle to 0
2. Change the position of 152 switch from 1 to 0.
3. Operate TE/BE handle normally and fine tuning is possible

@@3 13.9  Shunting mode
During Yard movements or shunting movements LP shall use shunting mode by keeping 160 switch to zero position in SB 1 panel. Preparation: Stop the train, Keep MPJ in 0, change the position of 160 switch from 1 to 0. Loco speed configured to 15KMPH maximum. While moving throttle to traction side every time F11 06 P2 message will appear. To normalize, Stop the train, Keep MPJ in 0, change the position of 160 switch from 0 to 1. Loco speed configured to MPS.

@@3 13.10  PTDC mode
In KNORR brake Locos, if F10 01 P1, Fault in Brake Electronics comes & even after troubleshooting unable to charge BP loco can be worked with PTDC mode to Clear section(s) with speed not exceeding 10KMPH. (Some E70 Locos also provided with PTDC) Preparation:
1. Switch off pneumatic panel MCB 127.7 in SB2 panel.
2. Turn PER-COS cock on pneumatic panel from horizontal to vertical position.
3. Move and hold the PTDC handle in active cab in the Release position. BP Pressure charges to 5 Kg/cm 2 .(Ensure PTDC handle in the rear cab is in LAP / CENTRE position)
4. BC Pressure should be released. If BC is not released then pull the quick release handle at bottom of C3W distributor valve, if unsuccessful (after securing the train) press TP16 / TP20 test nipples in Pneumatic panel.
5. Ensure BP drops by moving PTDC handle towards application position(spring loaded) & after releasing BP pressure charge to 5Kg/cm 2
6. Move TE/BE to traction side, ensure FLG596, Both TE/BE meters deviate and KN in display.
7. Clear section(s) with speed not exceeding 10KMPH.
8. SA9 handle will not function and A9 handle will function only at EMERGENCY Position.
!note Note:
1. TE/BE available only up to 10KMPH.
2. Before Releasing Loco brake, secure the train to avoid roll back.
3. Ensure C3W DV is in service in case of LE movement.

@@3 13.11  Dead man’s mode
If VCD foot switch/BPVG remains pressed for more than 60 seconds, then the system will go automatically to dead man mode, and buzzer will start, if BPVG / VFS not released with next 8 seconds, VCD penalty brakes will be applied. BP drops 2.5 to 3.0 kg/cm 2 .

@@3 13.12  Simulation mode
In the simulation mode the pantograph will not be raised. A special signal is sent to both SLGs to simulate catenary voltage. Then VCB can be closed and all operations can be performed in this mode.

@@3 13.13  DPWCS mode
Please refer chapter 14.1

@@3 13.14  PUSH-PULL mode
Please refer chapter 14.2

@@2 14. DPWCS and Push-Pull | ch=14
DISTRIBUTED POWER WIRELESS CONTROL SYSTEM OPERATING PROCEDURE & PUSH-PULL OPERATION

@@3 14.1  INAUGURATION PROCEDURE
1. CONFIGURE THE SLAVE LOCO FIRST
1. Proceed to Slave Loco.
2. Switch on the MCBs of DPWCS Equipment in SB2 panel.
3. Keep the Loco Brake Handle (SA 9) in the Release Position.
4. Insert BL Key in OFF and turn to D wait for FLG504.
5. Press DPWCS Mode in DIU, Enter Password 14488, LP ID and train number in DIU
6. Select Zone if any or select default.
7. Press Slave and then Press Enter, Press Slave position from given four slaves.
8. Remove the BL Key in the OFF Position
9. Proceed towards Master Loco.
2. CONFIGURE THE MASTER LOCO
1. Proceed to Master Loco.
2. Switch on the MCBs of DPWCS equipment in SB2 panel.
3. Insert BL Key in OFF and turn to D wait for FLG504.
4. Press DPWCS Mode in DIU, Enter Password 14488, LP ID and train number in DIU.
5. Select Zone if any or select default.
6. Press Master and then Press Enter.
7. Wait for the Slave Loco Number appears on Master Loco Display.
8. If the slave loco number appeared on the display is correct,
9. Press Enter for Slave Loco select.
3. SELECT WAGON TYPE & NUMBER OF WAGONS (IN MASTER)
1. Select any one type of wagon (BOXN,BOB-N,TBT etc), enter the number of wagons and press Enter
2. After entering all the data of wagons between master and first slave Press “Enter”.
3. If train is having more than one slave follow the above procedure.
4. After entering the wagons verify the train length and press enter for next process.
5. Raise the Panto and close the VCB.
6. Wait till BP to increase 4.8 Kg in master and slave.
7. Once BP reached 4.8 Kg, automatic drop carried by slave which reflects in
8. Master and Home screen will appear.
NEUTRAL SECTION NEGOTIATION At neutral sections, special operating procedure is needed for opening and closing the VCBs of slave locomotives. PROCEDURE FOR NEUTRAL SECTION
1. 100 meters before the open DJ board, Press Neutral Section button in DIU and then open BLDJ.
2. You will see message in display “neutral section in progress”.
3. After neutral section, close the VCB one mast after close DJ Board.
4. You will see message in display “Slave VCB will be OFF near neutral section”
5. If given command is proper, then Slave loco will get tripped near neutral section.
6. If you don’t find the message, it means that Neutral OFF switch activation has not been acknowledged by the system. Under this circumstances, the master and slave loco VCBs may trip/close together under synchronous mode.
7. If such a situation is encountered then press EMG hard key in DIU and quickly change to <IND> mode and operate the slave loco VCB independently at neutral section. After passing neutral section, the mode can be changed to synchronous. The slave loco VCB can be operated under IND mode.
8. Make sure while using independent mode that slave loco VCB must be opened before the neutral section.
9. If Slave Loco VCB does not get closed, one mast after close DJ board, then press EMG in DIU hard key and open the VCB of master loco and then close the VCB of master loco.
PANTO LOWERING NEGOTIATION At Panto lowering sections, special operating procedure is needed for lowering and raising the panto of slave locomotives. PROCEDURE FOR PANTO LOWERING
1. One mast before the Lowering section, Press Panto DOWN in DIU and then open BLDJ and lower the pantograph.
2. You will see message in display “Panto Lowering in progress”.
3. After Panto lowering section, Raise the pantograph and close the VCB after close DJ Board.
4. You will see message in display “Panto of Slave will be lower near Lowering section”.
5. If given command is proper, then Slave loco will get tripped near Lowering section.
6. If you don’t find the message, it means that Panto lowering switch activation has not been acknowledged by the system. Under this circumstance, the master and slave loco Panto may trip/close together under synchronous mode.
7. If such a situation is encountered, quickly change to <IND> mode and operate the slave loco independently at Lowering section. After passing Lowering section, the mode can be changed to synchronous.
8. Make sure while using independent mode that slave loco Panto must be lowered before the Lowering section.
9. If Slave Loco Panto does not get Raised, one mast after the lowering section, then lower the Panto in master loco and then Raise the panto in master loco.
PROCEDURE FOR RESTARTING SLAVE LOCO If there is a need to restart the slave loco, then Bring slave node to 504 and Press “REBOOT” then press LOCO and then press respected slave and enter in master DIU then Slave loco will get restarted.
PROCEDURE FOR SELECTING MODES INDEPENDENT MODE ACTIVATION Press “MODES “button in Master DIU Home screen.
Select “IND” button and Press respected slave and press ENTER.
Independent Selected slave will have highlighted with red box.
Independently control the Panto, VCB and throttle of the slave locomotives by given switches in the DIU.
ISOLATION MODE ACTIVATION Press “MODES “button in Master DIU Home screen.
Select “ISO” button and Press respected slave and press ENTER.
IDLE MODE ACTIVATION Press “MODES “button in Master DIU Home screen.
Select “IDLE” button and Press respected slave and press ENTER.
BRAKE MODE ACTIVATION Press “MODES “ button in Master DIU Home screen.
Select “BRAKE” button and Press respected slave and press ENTER.
BRAKE_OUT MODE ACTIVATION Press “MODES “button in Master DIU Home screen.
Select “BRAKE_OUT” button and Press respected slave and press ENTER.
Fault Troubleshooting
1. In case of any wrong command during neutral section or Panto lowering section press EMG hard key in DIU and operate the section independently.
2. If slave loco VCB gets tripped due to any loco related fault then acknowledge the fault by pressing BPFA in master and try to open and close the Master VCB.
3. If Slave and Master Locomotives are required to restart due to some loco fault, then restart the slave loco by pressing loco REBOOT in master DIU and once the slave loco restarted then restart the master loco manually.
4. If both master and slave locomotive restarted at once then Inauguration procedure must be carried out.
5. Due to loco fault, if slave loco must restart then restart the slave from master by operating reboot in master DIU.

@@3 14.2  PUSH-PULL Operation
In Push Pull mode one loco is in front of rake (called Master loco) and another loco is in rear of rake (called slave loco). Working principle is as like as MU operation.
The modification for push pull operation involves provision of additional BL switch, additional BPFA and additional LSDJ on ‘A’ panel in each cab of both master and slave loco.
ZTEL switch provided on ‘A’ panel is renamed as ZNN switch and is used for negotiating of neutral section.
COC/ Switch Master Master loco Slave loco
Panto selector switch 85 Auto Auto mode* Auto Mode
Feed pipe charging cock 136 Open Close
BP charging cock 70 Open Open
Emergency exhaust cock 74 Open Open
Dead movement cock 47 Close Close
Kaba key IG 38 Open Open
1. In Master loco pantograph selector switch 85 to be manually selected so that Rear pantograph is raised.
2. Connect BP and FP pipe of both locos with BP & FP pipe with train rake and open angle cocks.
In slave loco ensure: a) Keep SA-9 in released position in both cabs and observe ‘0’ Pressure in BC gauge. b) Move A-9 /DBC to emergency position and brakes will be applied in loco and rake. c) Move A-9 / DBC to neutral position and take out the handle. In master loco: a) Insert BL key in additional BL switch provided on ‘D’ panel and move it from OFF to D mode. This will result in glowing of additional LSDJ. (This amounts to activating slave loco)
b) After around 35-40 seconds slave loco will be on FLG504 and additional BPFA provided on D panel in master loco glows. (This amounts to VCU boot up in slave loco)
c) Move additional BL key from D to OFF and take it out. This will display FLG612 for slave loco in DDU of slave loco. (This amounts to slave loco in “self hold mode active”.
d) Insert BL key in ‘A’ panel and move from OFF to D and wait for 35-40 seconds. DDU will display FLG504 for both master and slave loco in Node information screen.
e) Now energise the locomotive pair of push pull combination from master loco as is done normally for single WAP-7 loco. Push pull combination of locos can be checked from train configuration screen.
f) If ZNN switch is on OFF position then VCBs of both master and slave loco will open or close simultaneously by BLDJ switch.
g) Node information displayed on DDU of master loco is for both Master and Slave loco. The status of slave loco is known from the node information displayed on DDU of master loco in column of slave loco.
Instructions for neutral section: a) At 500 m board by following signal aspects and section speed restrictions, attain maximum speed. b) At 250 m board bring throttle to Zero and switch ON ZNN. c) At DJ open board, switch OFF BLDJ and ensure LSDJ is glowing. d) At DJ close board, switch on BLDJ and ensure extinguishing of LSDJ. e) Additional BLDJ provided on ‘D’ panel of master loco will remain in extinguished condition continuously. f) The logic used for negotiating of neutral section in push pull operation is as i. After DJ OFF of master loco, DJ of slave loco opens after 330 meters. (Considering minimum rake length) ii. After DJ ON of master loco DJ of slave loco closes after 750 meters. (Considering maximum rake length)
!note Note: Opening and closing of VCB in slave loco can be checked from Node information screen in Master loco.
Troubleshooting
1. In Push Pull operation no fault messages will be pop up on DDU of slave loco. However fault messages will be recorded in slave loco and can be seen in DDS. (Diagnostic Data Set)
2. In Push Pull operation fault messages will be pop up on DDU of master loco with LSFI glowing but along with loco No, from loco no. LP has to
understand whether fault is in master loco or slave loco and take corrective action as per TSD.
3. In case of communication failure between master and slave loco message of “COMMUNICATION DISTURBANCE” is displayed in DDU of master loco and following action take place automatically. i) VCB tripping in master loco ii) VCB tripping and lowering of Panto in slave loco.
4. Master loco can be worked normally by closing VCB.
5. After communication failure and tripping of VCB and lowering of Pantograph in slave loco, it remains in self hold mode for 1 Hour. only.
6. During this one hour period only reconfigure master and slave loco combination at any convenient halt location etc. otherwise slave loco is to be made dead as per TSD at convenient halt station or having unscheduled halt.
7. In any case either reconfiguration or making of slave loco dead is to be done within one hour of self hold mode. Otherwise emergency brakes will be applied in train from slave loco halting train at any inconvenient location.
8. In Push pull operation Hotel load converters of master loco, if provided, will be in service. Therefore IVC coupler & UIC coupler of Hotel load of master loco only, to be provided with jumpers from front power car. No IVC & UIC jumper of hotel load converter to be connected between slave loco & rear power car.

@@2 15. WAP 5 Disc Brake | ch=15
Disc Brake in WAP 5 Loco
Parking Brake in WAP5 Locos: In WAP5 parking brake cylinders are available in wheel numbers 1,4,5 and 8. With the built-in brake cylinder and in conjunction with the wheel-mounted brake disc (set), the brake caliper constitutes a wheel disc brake (5.0 Kg/cm 2 on disc). This brake serves as a service brake on rail vehicles. Using spring brake cylinders, the brake caliper can also be applied as holding or parking brake.
Normal operation of parking brake is same as WAG9 loco (SV30). However some basic Trouble shooting points are given below.
S.No. Problem Cause Trouble Shooting 1 Conjunction Brakes does not work
Check the BC pressure and Distributor valve in service
Normalise same if DV isolated & try
2 Any one Brake cylinder not responding
Brake cylinder defective or Brake lever or pin broken
Secure it and inform to TLC
3 Occurrence of Untypical noises Lack of grease or wear limits are exceeded Inform to TLC
4 Brake binding Brake tongs are jammed. Checking of clearance brake pads-brake discs: The two brake pads of the brake caliper must have an equal clearance of 2 mm each towards the brake disc or a 4 mm clearance for one brake pad when the other is in contact
Try by manual releasing of Parking brakes. If not possible contact TLC for maintenance staff to readjust the affected brake cylinders.
